\textstyle{\widehat{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{Gr}}(T^{*}M)}

The vertical arrows are the quotient maps for the free and proper action of the compact Lie group
G
on
Q
, while the top horizontal arrow is a
G
-equivariant proper embedding. Hence,
\widehat{M}
inherits a quotient smooth structure such that the bottom horizontal arrow is a proper embedding.

Since the projection
{\mathrm{Gr}}_{r}(T^{*}M)\to M
is proper, the map
{\mathscr{R}}:\widehat{M}\to M
is smooth and proper. The connecteness of the fibers follows from the definition of the topology on
\widehat{M}
.

For the smoothness of the action we use again the Hamiltonian local model. In this model the action becomes:

\textstyle{(Q\mathchoice{\times^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.93613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.93613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.7pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mathfrak{g}^{*}}}}}{\times^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.93613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.93613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.7pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mathfrak{g}^{*}}}}}{\times^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.93613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.93613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.7pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mathfrak{g}^{*}}}}}{\times^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.93613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.93613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.7pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mathfrak{g}^{*}}}}}Q)/G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{}\ignorespaces(Q\mathchoice{\times^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.93613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.93613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.7pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mathfrak{g}^{*}}}}}{\times^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.93613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.93613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.7pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mathfrak{g}^{*}}}}}{\times^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.93613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.93613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.7pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mathfrak{g}^{*}}}}}{\times^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.93613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.93613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.7pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mathfrak{g}^{*}}}}}\widehat{\mathfrak{g}^{*}})/G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\circlearrowright

\textstyle{Q/G}

where the action is the obvious one.

Finally, from the definition of the action, its isotropy groups have constant dimension equal to
\operatorname{corank}\pi
, so the result follows.
∎

Remark 4.5
.

From the proof above we deduce the following property of the smooth structure of
\widehat{M}
. If
M\supset U\simeq Q/G
is a domain where the Hamiltonian local model holds, then
\widehat{U}:={\mathscr{R}}^{-1}(U)
is diffeomorphic to
\widehat{Q}/G
where
\widehat{Q}:=Q\mathchoice{\times^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.93613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.93613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.7pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mathfrak{g}^{*}}}}}{\times^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.93613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.93613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.7pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mathfrak{g}^{*}}}}}{\times^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.93613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.93613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.7pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mathfrak{g}^{*}}}}}{\times^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.93613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.93613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.7pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mathfrak{g}^{*}}}}}\widehat{\mathfrak{g}^{*}}
. Moreover, we have the following morphism of Morita equivalences,

\textstyle{\widehat{Q}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

=Q\times_{\mathfrak{g}^{*}}\widehat{\mathfrak{g}^{*}}

\scriptstyle{{\mathrm{pr}}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}}}
\scriptstyle{{\mathrm{pr}}_{Q}}
\textstyle{\widehat{M}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\widehat{Q}/G=

\scriptstyle{{\mathscr{R}}}
\textstyle{Q\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p}
\scriptstyle{\mu}
\textstyle{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}^{*}}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

=G/T\times_{W}\mathfrak{t}^{*}

\scriptstyle{{\mathscr{R}}}
\textstyle{M{}\ignorespaces}

Q/G=

\textstyle{\mathfrak{g}^{*}}

where the bottom two-leg diagram is an equivalence between
\mathcal{G}
and
G\ltimes\mathfrak{g}^{*}
, and the top two-leg diagram is an equivalence between the action groupoids
\mathcal{G}\ltimes\widehat{M}
and
G\ltimes\widehat{\mathfrak{g}^{*}}
. We will be using this local model for
\widehat{M}
in the sequel.

Recall that
y\in Y
is a clean value of a smooth map
\phi:X\to Y
if the preimage
\phi^{-1}(y)
is a submanifold of
X
and
T_{x}\phi^{-1}(y)=\Ker\mathrm{d}_{x}\phi
for all
x\in\phi^{-1}(y)
.

Proposition 4.6
.

Let
(M,\pi)
be a Poisson manifold of proper type. The differential at
{\widehat{x}}:=(x,\mathfrak{t}_{x})\in\widehat{M}
of the resolution map
{\mathscr{R}}
has range the subspace
\mathcal{R}_{{\widehat{x}}}\subset T_{x}M
whose annihilator is

(\mathcal{R}_{{\widehat{x}}})^{0}=[\mathfrak{t}_{x},\mathfrak{g}_{x}]\subset T_{x}^{*}M.

Furthermore,

\dim(\Ker\mathrm{d}_{{\widehat{x}}}{\mathscr{R}})=\dim\mathfrak{g}_{x}-\dim\mathfrak{t}_{x},

and all values of
{\mathscr{R}}
are clean.

Proof.

Since this result is local, we can assume that
M=Q/G
for a Hamiltonian local model
\mu:(Q,\omega)\to\mathfrak{g}^{*}
. We also observe that we can reduce the result to the linear case due to the following diagram:

\textstyle{T^{*}_{(x,\mathfrak{t}_{x})}\widehat{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{T_{(q,x,\mathfrak{t}_{x})}^{*}(Q\mathchoice{\times^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.3764pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.3764pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.2879pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.77707pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{M}}}}{\times^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.3764pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.3764pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.2879pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.77707pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{M}}}}{\times^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.3764pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.3764pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.2879pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.77707pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{M}}}}{\times^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.3764pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.3764pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.2879pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.77707pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{M}}}}\widehat{M})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{T_{(q,\xi,\mathfrak{t}_{\xi})}^{*}(Q\mathchoice{\times^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.93613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.93613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.7pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mathfrak{g}^{*}}}}}{\times^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.93613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.93613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.7pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mathfrak{g}^{*}}}}}{\times^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.93613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.93613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.7pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mathfrak{g}^{*}}}}}{\times^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.93613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.93613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.7pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mathfrak{g}^{*}}}}}\widehat{\mathfrak{g}^{*}})}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces T^{*}_{(\xi,\mathfrak{t}_{\xi})}\widehat{\mathfrak{g}^{*}}}
\textstyle{T^{*}_{x}M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{p}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{{\mathscr{R}}^{*}}
\textstyle{T_{q}^{*}Q\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathrm{pr}}_{Q}^{*}}
\textstyle{T^{*}_{q}Q\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathrm{pr}}_{Q}^{*}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces T^{*}_{\xi}\mathfrak{g}^{*}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu^{*}}
\scriptstyle{{\mathscr{R}}^{*}}

where we fixed a point
(q,x,\mathfrak{t}_{x})\in Q\mathchoice{\times^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.3764pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.3764pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.2879pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.77707pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{M}}}}{\times^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.3764pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.3764pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.2879pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.77707pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{M}}}}{\times^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.3764pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.3764pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.2879pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.77707pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{M}}}}{\times^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.3764pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.3764pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.2879pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.77707pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{M}}}}\widehat{M}
,
\xi=\mu(q)
, and
\phi_{q}(\mathfrak{t}_{\xi})=\mathfrak{t}_{x}
where
\phi_{q}:\mathfrak{g}_{\xi}\to\mathfrak{g}^{M}_{x}
is the isomorphism (
4.10
). The kernels of the vertical arrows are contained in various isotropy Lie algebras that sit inside the bottom row and correspond to each other

\textstyle{\mathfrak{g}^{M}_{x}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{p^{*}(\mathfrak{g}^{M}_{x})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mu^{*}(\mathfrak{g}^{M}_{x})}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathfrak{g}_{\xi}}

In other words, we have:

(\mathcal{R}_{(x,{\widehat{x}})})^{0}=\phi_{q}((\mathcal{R}_{\xi,\mathfrak{t}_{\xi}})^{0}),

where on the right side we have the range of the differential of
{\mathscr{R}}:\widehat{\mathfrak{g}^{*}}\to\mathfrak{g}^{*}
.

We are left with proving the statement for the linear case. We use the model (
4.8
) for the resolution map

\textstyle{G\times\mathfrak{t}^{*}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{{\mathscr{R}}}}
\textstyle{G/T\mathchoice{\times^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.59724pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.59724pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.30833pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.79167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{W}}}}{\times^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.59724pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.59724pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.30833pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.79167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{W}}}}{\times^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.59724pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.59724pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.30833pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.79167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{W}}}}{\times^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.59724pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.59724pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.30833pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.79167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{W}}}}\mathfrak{t}^{*}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathscr{R}}}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{g}^{*}}

By
G
equivariance, it suffices to describe the image of
\mathrm{d}\widetilde{{\mathscr{R}}}
at elements
(1,\xi)
. Since

\mathrm{d}_{(1,\xi)}\widetilde{{\mathscr{R}}}(u,\eta)=\ad_{u}\xi+\eta,

it follows that its image is annihilated precisely by
[\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{g}_{\xi}]
(note that
\mathfrak{t}\subset\mathfrak{g}_{\xi}
), proving the statement about the range.

The last formula in the statement now follows from
\mathfrak{g}_{x}=\mathfrak{t}_{x}\oplus[\mathfrak{t}_{x},\mathfrak{g}_{x}]
. Also, since the preimage
{\mathscr{R}}^{-1}(x)=\mathcal{T}(\mathfrak{g}_{x})\subset{\mathrm{Gr}}_{r}(T_{x}M)
is a submanifold and
T{\mathscr{R}}^{-1}(x)\subset\Ker\mathrm{d}{\mathscr{R}}
, by a dimension count it follows that
x
is a clean value of
{\mathscr{R}}
.
∎

Corollary 4.7
.

Let
(M,\pi)
be a Poisson manifold of proper type and
(\mathcal{G},\Omega)\rightrightarrows M
a proper integration. Then

(4.11)

(\mathcal{R}_{{\widehat{x}}})^{0}\cap\Ker\widehat{\mathscr{a}}=\{0\},

where
\widehat{\mathscr{a}}:{\mathscr{R}}^{*}T^{*}M\to T\widehat{M}
is the infinitesimal action
induced by the action (
4.1
).

4.2.
Dirac versus Poisson geometry

The local model for the resolution
\widehat{M}
, as in Remark
4.5
, can be enhanced with a presymplectic form. If
M=Q/G
for a Hamiltonian
G
-space
\mu:(Q,\omega)\to\mathfrak{g}^{*}
, then
\widehat{\omega}={\mathrm{pr}}^{*}_{Q}\omega
gives the following presymplectic Hamiltonian
G
- space:

(4.12)

\textstyle{(\widehat{Q},\widehat{\omega})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathrm{pr}}_{Q}}
\scriptstyle{\widehat{\mu}}
\textstyle{(Q,\omega)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{g}^{*}}
\textstyle{\widehat{M}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\widehat{Q}/G=

\textstyle{M{}\ignorespaces}

=Q/G

Since
\widehat{\omega}
has kernel, this brings us to the Dirac geometry of the resolution.

Theorem 4.8
.

Let
(M,\pi)
be a Poisson manifold of proper type. Then
\widehat{M}
carries a unique Dirac structure
\widehat{L}_{\pi}
making
{\mathscr{R}}:\widehat{M}\to M
a forward Dirac map. Furthermore:

(i)

\widehat{L}_{\pi}
is a regular Dirac structure;

(ii)

the presymplectic leaves
(\widehat{S},\omega_{\widehat{S}})
of
\widehat{L}_{\pi}
take the form

\widehat{S}={\mathscr{R}}^{-1}(S),\quad\omega_{\widehat{S}}={\mathscr{R}}^{*}(\omega_{S}),

where
(S,\omega_{S})
is a symplectic leaf of
(M,\pi)
;

(iii)

the Poisson locus of
\widehat{L}_{\pi}
is
{\mathscr{R}}^{-1}(M^{{\mathrm{reg}}})
and
{\mathscr{R}}:{\mathscr{R}}^{-1}(M^{{\mathrm{reg}}})\to M^{\mathrm{reg}}
is a diffeomorphism.

Moreover,
{\mathscr{R}}
induces a homeomorphism between the leaf spaces associated to
\widehat{L}_{\pi}
and
\pi
.

Proof.

We define
\widehat{L}_{\pi}
as in (
4.2
) by pulling back the Poisson structure
\pi
viewed as a Dirac structure. Hence,
\widehat{L}_{\pi}
is given pointwise by

\widehat{L}_{\pi}|_{{\widehat{x}}}=\{(V,{\mathscr{R}}^{*}\beta):\pi^{\sharp}(\beta)=\mathrm{d}_{{\widehat{x}}}{\mathscr{R}}(V)\}.

In order to obtain a Dirac structure, we only need to check that this is a smooth vector subbundle of
T\widehat{M}\oplus T^{*}\widehat{M}
(see, e.g.,
[
13
, Section 7.3]
). To that end, we observe that
\widehat{L}_{\pi}
has rank equal to
\dim M
and contains the image of the injective bundle map

(4.13)

(\widehat{\mathscr{a}},\mathrm{d}{\mathscr{R}}^{*}):{\mathscr{R}}^{*}T^{*}M\to T\widehat{M}\oplus T^{*}\widehat{M}.

Here
\widehat{\mathscr{a}}:{\mathscr{R}}^{*}T^{*}M\to T\widehat{M}
is the infinitesimal action corresponding to the action of
\mathcal{G}
on
\widehat{M}
, and injectivity follows from Corollary
4.7
. In conclusion,
\widehat{L}_{\pi}
is the image of the previous injective bundle map.

The description of the presymplectic leaves in item (ii) follows from the fact that
{\mathscr{R}}
is a backward Dirac map.

To prove item (iii), observe that
{\mathscr{R}}:{\mathscr{R}}^{-1}(M^{{\mathrm{reg}}})\to M^{\mathrm{reg}}
is a bijection and by the construction of the smooth structure it follows that it is a diffeomorphism. Since
M^{\mathrm{reg}}
is open and dense in
M
and
{\mathscr{R}}
is proper, we have that
{\mathscr{R}}^{-1}(M^{{\mathrm{reg}}})
is open and dense in
\widehat{M}
. From this it follows that
{\mathscr{R}}
is also a forward Dirac map, and that the Dirac structure is unique.

Since
{\mathscr{R}}
is a proper map, it follows that the continuous bijection between leaf spaces is also a closed map, hence a homeomorphism.
∎

Corollary 4.9
.

Let
(M,\pi)
be a Poisson manifold of proper type. For
y\in\widehat{M}
the following are equivalent:

(a)

{\mathscr{R}}(y)
is a regular point of
(M,\pi)
;

(b)

{\mathscr{R}}(y)
is a regular value of
{\mathscr{R}}
;

(c)

y
belongs to the Poisson support of
\widehat{L}_{\pi}
.

Corollary 4.10
.

The restriction
{\mathscr{R}}:\widehat{S}\to S
is a fiber bundle.

Proof.

Proposition
4.6
shows that
T\mathcal{F}_{\pi}\subset{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathrm{d}{\mathscr{R}})
. Since the resolution map
{\mathscr{R}}
is a backward Dirac map, it follows that its restriction to a presymplectic leaf is automatically a submersion. The result then follows from the fact that
{\mathscr{R}}
is proper.
∎

Example 4.11
.

One can obtain a Hamiltonian local model for
(\widehat{M},\widehat{L}_{\pi})
from one for
(M,\mathcal{F}_{\pi})
. Assume that

M=Q/G
is a Hamiltonian
G
-space
\mu:(Q,\omega)\to\mathfrak{g}^{*}
. Fixing a maximal torus
T\subset G
, one has

(4.14)

\widehat{M}\simeq\mu^{-1}(\mathfrak{t}^{*})/N(T),

for the presymplectic Hamiltonian
N(T)
-space

\mu:(\mu^{-1}(\mathfrak{t}^{*}),i^{*}\omega)\to\mathfrak{t}^{*},

where
i:\mu^{-1}(\mathfrak{t}^{*})\hookrightarrow Q
is the inclusion. The identification (
4.14
) is given by

qN(T)\mapsto(\mathfrak{p}(q),\phi_{q}(\mathfrak{t})),

and arises from applying
Q\mathchoice{\times^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.93613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.93613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.7pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mathfrak{g}^{*}}}}}{\times^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.93613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.93613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.7pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mathfrak{g}^{*}}}}}{\times^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.93613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.93613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.7pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mathfrak{g}^{*}}}}}{\times^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.93613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.93613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.7pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mathfrak{g}^{*}}}}}-
to the transversal
\mathfrak{t}^{*}\hookrightarrow\widehat{\mathfrak{g}^{*}}
,
\xi\mapsto(\xi,\mathfrak{t})
.

Example 4.12
.

Consider the linear local model
(M,\pi^{\theta}_{\mathrm{lin}})
associated with the data of a principal
G
-bundle
p:P\to(S,\omega_{S})
and a connection 1-form
\theta\in\Omega^{1}(P,\mathfrak{g})
, described in Section
2.2
. One has
M=(P\times\mathfrak{g}^{*})/G
and the Dirac structure
L_{\pi^{\theta}_{\mathrm{lin}}}
is the reduction of the form (cf. (
2.5
)):

p^{*}\omega_{S}-\mathrm{d}\langle\theta,\cdot\rangle\in\Omega^{2}(P\times\mathfrak{g}^{*}).

Fixing a maximal torus
T\subset G
one obtains data for a linear local model for
(\widehat{M},\widehat{L}_{\pi})
. Namely:

•

the principal
N(T)
-bundle
q:P\to\widehat{S}=P/N(T)
;

•

the presymplectic form
{\mathscr{R}}^{*}\omega_{S}
on
\widehat{S}
;

•

the connection 1-form
\theta^{T}:={\mathrm{pr}}_{\mathfrak{t}}\circ\theta\in\Omega^{1}(P,\mathfrak{t})
which is the
\mathfrak{t}
-component of
\theta
with respect to the decomposition
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{t}\oplus[\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{g}]
.

The construction in Section
2.2
gives the local model for
\widehat{M}
together with a map induced by the inclusion
\mathfrak{t}^{*}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{g}^{*}
,

{\mathscr{R}}:\widehat{M}=(P\times\mathfrak{t}^{*})/N(T)\to M=(P\times\mathfrak{g}^{*})/G,\quad\quad[(p,\xi)]_{N(T)}\mapsto[(p,\xi)]_{G}.

The Dirac structure
\widehat{L}_{\pi^{\theta}_{\mathrm{lin}}}
for
\widehat{M}
is obtained as a quotient of the
N(T)
-invariant form (see

p^{*}\omega_{S}-\mathrm{d}\langle\theta^{T},\cdot\rangle\in\Omega^{2}(P\times\mathfrak{t}^{*}),

where we used that
q^{*}{\mathscr{R}}^{*}\omega_{s}=p^{*}\omega_{S}
.
The map

{\mathscr{R}}:(\widehat{M},\widehat{L}_{\pi^{\theta}_{\mathrm{lin}}})\to(M,{L}_{\pi^{\theta}_{\mathrm{lin}}})

is forward and backward Dirac, and it is the resolution for
(M,\pi^{\theta}_{\mathrm{lin}})
in our sense over the Poisson support of
{L}_{\pi^{\theta}_{\mathrm{lin}}}
.

Proposition 4.13
.

Let
(\mathcal{G},\Omega)
be an integration of
(M,\pi)
of (strong)
\mathcal{C}
-type and consider the
\mathcal{G}
-action on
\widehat{M}
. The associated action groupoid

\widehat{\mathcal{G}}:=\mathcal{G}\ltimes\widehat{M},\quad\widehat{\Omega}:={\mathrm{pr}}_{\mathcal{G}}^{*}\Omega,

is a presymplectic integration of
(\widehat{M},\widehat{L}_{\pi})
of (strong)
\mathcal{C}
-type.

Proof.

Since
{\mathrm{pr}}_{\mathcal{G}}:\mathcal{G}\ltimes\widehat{M}\to\mathcal{G}
is a groupoid morphism, the form
\widehat{\Omega}
is a closed, multiplicative 2-form. The only things to be checked are that (i) the non-degeneracy condition

\Ker\mathrm{d}\mathbf{s}\cap\Ker\mathrm{d}\mathbf{t}\cap\Ker\widehat{\Omega}=\{0\}

holds and (ii)
\widehat{L}_{\pi}
is the induced Dirac structure on the base. Both of these follow from
[
8
, Corollary 4.8]
and the fact that (
4.13
) is bundle isomorphism onto
\widehat{L}_{\pi}
.

Since
{\mathscr{R}}:\widehat{M}\to M
is a proper map,
\mathcal{G}\ltimes\widehat{M}
has the same
\mathcal{C}
-type as
\mathcal{G}
.
∎

4.3.
A characterization of the Weyl resolution

We provide a way to detect the Weyl resolution
among backward Dirac maps.

Theorem 4.14
.

Let
\phi:(N,L)\to(M,\pi)
be a backward Dirac map from a Dirac manifold to a Poisson manifold of proper type with the following properties:

(i)

L
is regular;

(ii)

\phi
is proper and restricts to a diffeomorphism

\phi:\phi^{-1}(M^{\mathrm{reg}})\to M^{\mathrm{reg}}.

(iii)

There is an action
\widehat{\mathscr{a}}
of
T^{*}M
on
\phi
(necessarily unique)
yielding a Lie algebroid isomorphism
(\widehat{\mathscr{a}},\phi^{*}):\phi^{*}T^{*}M\to L
;

Then there exist a diffeomorphism

\psi:(N,L)\to(\widehat{M},\widehat{L}_{\pi})

such that
\phi={\mathscr{R}}\circ\psi
.

Remark 4.15
.

The previous theorem implies that following global statement. Assume that
(\mathcal{G},\Omega)\rightrightarrows(M,\pi)
is a proper groupoid integrating a Poisson manifold
(M,\pi)
, acting along a proper map
\phi:N\to M
, such that:

(i)

the orbits of the action are regular;

(ii)

\phi
restricts to a diffeomorphism

\phi:\phi^{-1}(M^{\mathrm{reg}})\to M^{\mathrm{reg}};

(iii)

the resulting action groupoid, endowed with the pull-back of
\Omega
by the projection, is a presymplectic groupoid.

Then the same conclusion of the theorem holds.

Proof.

The map
\phi
is backward Dirac, hence
L=\phi^{!}L_{\pi}
. In order to define the map
\psi:N\to\widehat{M}
we denote by
\mathcal{I}\to N
the bundle of isotropy Lie algebras of
\phi^{!}L_{\pi}
. This is a bundle of abelian Lie algebras, because
\phi^{!}L_{\pi}
is regular, and we consider the composition of Lie algebroid morphisms

(4.15)

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 12.52779pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-12.52779pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\psi_{0}:\mathcal{I}\,\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.52779pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 36.52779pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 36.52779pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\phi^{!}L_{\pi}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 62.77925pt\raise 7.48611pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.73611pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{{(\widehat{\mathscr{a}},\phi)}^{-1}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 87.23192pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 87.23192pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\phi^{*}T^{*}M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 128.99927pt\raise 5.1875pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{{\mathrm{pr}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 150.40901pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 150.40901pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{T^{*}M}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

The last projection is injective on isotropy Lie algebras. Hence,
\psi_{0}
is a fiberwise injective vector bundle map covering
\phi:N\to M
. Therefore we have an induced smooth map between the Grassmannians of rank
r=\operatorname{corank}(\pi)
:

\psi:N={\mathrm{Gr}}_{r}(\mathcal{I})\to{\mathrm{Gr}}_{r}(T^{*}M),\quad y\mapsto\psi_{0}(\mathcal{I}_{y}).

Over the open dense set
\phi^{-1}(M^{\mathrm{reg}})
the map
\psi_{0}
is an isomorphism which takes values in
\widehat{M}\subset{\mathrm{Gr}}_{r}(T^{*}M)
. Hence, applying also Theorem
4.4
, one has that:

(a)

\widehat{M}
is closed in
{\mathrm{Gr}}_{r}(T^{*}M)
, so
\psi
has image contained in
\widehat{M}
;

(b)

\widehat{M}
is embedded in
{\mathrm{Gr}}_{r}(T^{*}M)
, so
\psi
can be viewed has a smooth map into
\widehat{M}
.

Therefore

\psi:N\to\widehat{M},\quad y\mapsto\psi_{0}(\mathcal{I}_{y})\in{\mathrm{Gr}}_{r}(T^{*}_{x}M)

is a smooth map that satisfies

(4.16)

{\mathscr{R}}\circ\psi=\phi.

Next we show that
\psi
is a diffeomorphism. For that it is enough to prove that it is a local diffeomorphism. Indeed, note that since
\phi
is a proper map, the factorization (
4.16
) implies that also
\psi
is proper. A proper local diffeomorphism is necessarily a covering map and
\psi
being both a closed and an open map, must be surjective. Since
\psi
is a diffeomorphism over
M^{\mathrm{reg}}
, we conclude that
\psi:N\to\widehat{M}
is a diffeomorphism.

Finally, to show that
\psi
is a local diffeomorphism we note that, by (
4.16
), we have

\Ker\psi_{*}\subset\Ker\phi_{*}.

Hence, injectivity of
\psi_{*}
follows from the identity

(4.17)

\Ker\phi_{*}\cap\Ker\psi_{*}=\{0\}.

To prove this identity, let
y\in N
and let
S^{\prime}\subset N
and
S\subset M
denote the presymplectic leaves through
y
and
x=\phi(y)
, respectively. Since
\phi
is a backward Dirac map, we have that
(X,0)\in\phi^{!}L_{\pi}
for any
X\in\Ker\phi_{*}
, so

{(\Ker\phi_{*})}|_{S^{\prime}}=\Ker{(\phi|_{S^{\prime}})}_{*}.

We now use the algebroid isomorphism
(\widehat{\mathscr{a}},\phi):\phi^{*}T^{*}M\to\phi^{!}L_{\pi}
. Since the algebroid
T^{*}M
acts along the proper map
\phi
, the action integrates to an action of the source 1-connected Lie groupoid
\Sigma(M)

[
33
, Thm 5.3]
. It follows that for any
x\in S

we obtain an action of the isotropy group
\Sigma(M)_{x}
on the fiber

\phi^{-1}(x)\cap S^{\prime}=(\phi|_{S^{\prime}})^{-1}(x)

whose orbits are tangent to
\Ker{(\phi|_{S^{\prime}})}_{*}
. This also shows that we have a
local
action of the isotropy group
G_{x}
of a (possible smaller) proper integration of
(M,\pi)
on this fiber. This provides a diffeomorphism

G_{x}/T_{x}\supset U\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\smash{$\sim$}}}V\subset{(\phi|_{S^{\prime}})}^{-1}(x)

from a neighborhood
U
of the coset of the identity to a neighborhood
V
of
y
in
(\phi|_{S^{\prime}})^{-1}(x)
. Under this isomorphism, the map
\psi|_{V}
becomes the restriction of the canonical covering map

G_{x}/T_{x}\supset U\to G_{x}/N(T_{x})=\mathcal{T}(\mathfrak{g}_{x}).

Therefore the differential of
\psi
along
\Ker\phi_{*}
is injective, and this proves (
4.17
).
∎

4.4.
Resolution and the infinitesimal stratification

In the sequel, we will say that the intersection of a map
\phi:X\to Y
with a (embedded) submanifold
S\subset Y
is
very clean
if it satisfies the following properties:

(a)

\phi^{-1}(S)
is a (embedded) submanifold of
X
;

(b)

T\phi^{-1}(S)=(\mathrm{d}\phi)^{-1}(TS)
;

(c)

\phi|_{\phi^{-1}(S)}:\phi^{-1}(S)\to S
is a submersion.

Notice that the usual notion of cleanness only requires the first two items which, unlike the notion of very clean, do not imply that points in
S
are clean values. Also, (c) guarantees that the rank of
\phi
is constant along
\phi^{-1}(S)
.

Cleanness is relevant also when trying to pull-back stratifications
\mathcal{S}_{Y}
of
Y
along
\phi:X\to Y
: if
\phi
intersects all the strata of
\mathcal{S}_{Y}
cleanly and
\phi
has connected fibers, then

\phi^{*}\mathcal{S}_{Y}:=\left\{\phi^{-1}(S):S\in\mathcal{S}_{Y}\right\}

is a stratification of
X
. Requirement (b) is relevant when addressing the Whitney conditions. Very clean ensures that the conclusion holds not only for
\mathcal{S}_{Y}
but for all other refinements of
\mathcal{S}_{Y}
.

Theorem 4.16
.

Let
(M,\pi)
be a Poisson manifold of proper type. Then for each stratum
\Sigma
of
\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{inf}}(M,\pi)
, one has:

(i)

The intersection of
{\mathscr{R}}
with
\Sigma
is very clean;

(ii)

{\mathscr{R}}^{-1}(\Sigma)
is an embedded submanifold of
\widehat{M}
along which
{\mathscr{R}}
has constant rank equal to
\dim M+\operatorname{corank}(\pi)-\dim\mathfrak{g}_{x}
, for any
x\in\Sigma
.

The partition of
\widehat{M}
given by the rank of
\mathrm{d}{\mathscr{R}}
induces the stratification
{\mathscr{R}}^{-1}(\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{inf}}(M,\pi))
, after passing to connected components.

Proof.

Let us assume, for the moment, that

(C)

{\mathscr{R}}^{-1}(\Sigma)
is embedded in
\widehat{M}
and the restriction
\mathrm{d}{\mathscr{R}}:T{\mathscr{R}}^{-1}(\Sigma)\to T\Sigma
is surjective.

Then we have an inclusion of short exact sequences

\textstyle{T_{{\widehat{x}}}{\mathscr{R}}^{-1}(x)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{T_{{\widehat{x}}}{\mathscr{R}}^{-1}(\Sigma)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{T_{x}\Sigma\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Ker(\mathrm{d}_{{\widehat{x}}}{\mathscr{R}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(\mathrm{d}_{{\widehat{x}}}{\mathscr{R}})^{-1}(T_{x}\Sigma)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{T_{x}\Sigma}

Since all values of
{\mathscr{R}}
are clean, the left vertical arrow is an equality, hence, so is the middle one. To complete the proof of the very clean statement (no pun intended) it remains to check that (C) holds.

In order to prove (C) we can use the Hamiltonian local model, so we assume
M=Q/G
for a Hamiltonian
G
-space
\mu:(Q,\omega)\to\mathfrak{g}^{*}
. According to (
4.14
) the resolution map reads

{\mathscr{R}}:\mu^{-1}(\mathfrak{t}^{*})/N(T)\to Q/G,

where
T\subset G
is a a fixed maximal torus. Then
\Sigma
is identified with (see (
3.12
))

\Sigma=\mu^{-1}(\mathfrak{z}(\mathfrak{g})^{*})/G,

while its pre-image becomes

{\mathscr{R}}^{-1}(\Sigma)=\mu^{-1}(\mathfrak{z}(\mathfrak{g})^{*})/N(T).

From this (C) is immediate.

To check that the preimage of
\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{inf}}(M,\pi)
is a stratification of
\widehat{M}
, one uses the local model, as in Remark
4.5
. This reduces the problem to the linear case where
{\mathscr{R}}:G/T\mathchoice{\times^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.59724pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.59724pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.30833pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.79167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{W}}}}{\times^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.59724pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.59724pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.30833pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.79167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{W}}}}{\times^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.59724pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.59724pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.30833pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.79167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{W}}}}{\times^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.59724pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.59724pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.30833pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.79167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{W}}}}\mathfrak{t}^{*}\to\mathfrak{g}^{*}
and the infinitesimal stratification of
\mathfrak{g}^{*}
has strata the
G
-saturations of the open faces
{\mathrm{int}}(\Delta_{I})
of a Weyl chamber
\Delta
of
\mathfrak{t}^{*}
(see Example
8.1
for more details). One observes that their pullbacks under the resolution map,
{\mathscr{R}}^{-1}({\mathrm{int}}(\Delta_{I}))=G/T\mathchoice{\times^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.59724pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.59724pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.30833pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.79167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{W}}}}{\times^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.59724pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.59724pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.30833pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.79167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{W}}}}{\times^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.59724pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.59724pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.30833pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.79167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{W}}}}{\times^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.59724pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.59724pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.30833pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.79167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{W}}}}(W\cdot{\mathrm{int}}(\Delta_{I}))
, satisfy the frontier condition.

The formula for the dimension in item (ii) now follows. From this formula and the characterization (
3.3
) of the infinitesimal stratification, applying Lemma
3.5
, the last statement also follows.
∎

Corollary 4.17
.

For
\Sigma\in\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{inf}}(M,\pi)
the following holds

\displaystyle\codim(\Sigma)-\codim({\mathscr{R}}^{-1}(\Sigma))

\displaystyle=\dim(\mathfrak{g}_{x})-\dim(\mathfrak{t}_{x}),

\displaystyle\codim({\mathscr{R}}^{-1}(\Sigma))

\displaystyle=\dim(\mathfrak{t}_{x})-\dim(\mathfrak{z}(\mathfrak{g}_{x})).

In particular, for any subregular stratum
\Sigma
,

{\mathscr{R}}^{-1}(\Sigma)
is of codimension one.

4.5.
Resolution and the canonical stratification

A similar result holds for the pullback of the canonical stratification.
This time however,
(\widehat{M},\widehat{L}_{\pi})
comes with its own
canonical stratification, denoted by
\mathcal{S}(\widehat{M},\widehat{L}_{\pi})
, as follows from the results in Section
3.4
.
However, since
\widehat{L}_{\pi}
is regular, Proposition
3.22
allows us to be more direct and adopt the following:

(4.18)

\mathcal{S}(\widehat{M},\widehat{L}_{\pi}):=\mathcal{S}(\widehat{M},\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{\pi}),

the canonical stratification of the underlying regular foliation, as discussed in section
3.1.4
.
More transparently, the analogue of Proposition
3.23
ensures that

\mathcal{S}(\widehat{M},\widehat{L}_{\pi})
coincides with the canonical stratification induced by its integrating groupoid
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}\rightrightarrows\widehat{M}
,
as discussed in section
3.1.3
.

Theorem 4.18
.

Let
(M,\pi)
be a Poisson manifold of proper type. The resolution map

{\mathscr{R}}:(\widehat{M},\widehat{L}_{\pi})\to(M,\pi)

is compatible with the canonical stratifications. More precisely,
{\mathscr{R}}
intersects every canonical stratum very cleanly and

\mathcal{S}(\widehat{M},\widehat{L}_{\pi})=\left\{{\mathscr{R}}^{-1}(\mathfrak{S}):\mathfrak{S}\in\mathcal{S}(M,\pi)\right\}

Proof.

We claim that
{\mathscr{R}}^{-1}(\mathcal{S}(M,\pi))
is a stratification of
\widehat{M}
with the property that

(4.19)

T_{y}\widehat{\mathfrak{S}}=T_{y}{\mathscr{R}}^{-1}(\mathfrak{S}),

for every
\widehat{\mathfrak{S}}\in\mathcal{S}(\widehat{M},\widehat{L}_{\pi})
,
\mathfrak{S}\in\mathcal{S}(M,\pi)
and
y\in\widehat{\mathfrak{S}}\cap{\mathscr{R}}^{-1}(\mathfrak{S})
. Applying Lemma
3.6
, the result follows.

To prove the claim we first observe that that
{\mathscr{R}}^{-1}(\mathcal{S}(M,\pi))
is a stratification because
\mathcal{S}(M,\pi)
refines
\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{inf}}(M,\pi)
,
and
{\mathscr{R}}
is a map with connected fibers which intersects very cleanly the strata of
\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{inf}}(M,\pi)
– see the remarks before Theorem
4.16
.

To prove (
4.19
) fix
{\widehat{x}}=(x,\mathfrak{t}_{x})
and consider the stratum
\mathfrak{S}\in\mathcal{S}(M,\pi)
through
x
and the stratum
\widehat{\mathfrak{S}}\in\mathcal{S}(\widehat{M},\widehat{L}_{\pi})
through
{\widehat{x}}
. We also fix the symplectic leaf
S\subset M
through
x
and the presymplectic leaf
\widehat{S}\subset\widehat{M}
through
{\widehat{x}}
. Since
\widehat{S}={\mathscr{R}}^{-1}(S)
, (
4.19
) will follow by showing that:

(4.20)

T_{{\widehat{x}}}\widehat{\mathfrak{S}}/T_{{\widehat{x}}}\widehat{S}=T_{{\widehat{x}}}{\mathscr{R}}^{-1}(\mathfrak{S})/T_{{\widehat{x}}}\widehat{S}.

For this, observe that
{\mathscr{R}}
intersects
S
cleanly, so we have an injection of normal spaces:

\mathrm{d}_{{\widehat{x}}}{\mathscr{R}}:T_{{\widehat{x}}}\widehat{M}/T_{{\widehat{x}}}\widehat{S}\to T_{x}M/T_{x}S=\mathfrak{g}^{*}_{x}.

By Proposition
4.6
, the image is
\mathfrak{t}^{*}_{x}
, and we will identify the normal space to
\widehat{S}
with this image:

(4.21)

\mathrm{d}_{{\widehat{x}}}{\mathscr{R}}:T_{{\widehat{x}}}\widehat{M}/T_{{\widehat{x}}}\widehat{S}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathfrak{t}^{*}_{x}\subset\mathfrak{g}^{*}_{x}=T_{x}M/T_{x}S.

Now we fix a integration
(\mathcal{G},\Omega)
of
(M,\pi)
, we let
G=G_{x}
and
T\subset G
be the maximal torus of
G
with Lie algebra
\mathfrak{t}=\mathfrak{t}_{x}
. From now on, we also denote
\mathfrak{g}_{x}
by
\mathfrak{g}
. For the associated action groupoid
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}=\mathcal{G}\ltimes\widehat{M}
the isotropy group at
(x,\mathfrak{t})
is
N(T)
. Its isotropy Lie algebra
\mathfrak{t}
is canonically identified with the conormal to
\widehat{S}
at
(x,\mathfrak{t})
. This is precisely the dual identification to (
4.21
). We need the following lemma which identifies each side of (
4.20
).

Lemma 4.19
.

Under the identification
4.21
, one has that:

(i)

the right-hand side of (
4.20
) becomes

T_{(x,\mathfrak{t})}{\mathscr{R}}^{-1}(\mathfrak{S})/T_{(x,\mathfrak{t})}\widehat{S}=(\mathfrak{z}(\mathfrak{g})^{*})^{G/G^{0}}\subset\mathfrak{t}^{*};

(ii)

the left-hand side of (
4.20
) becomes

T_{(x,\mathfrak{t})}\widehat{\mathfrak{S}}/T_{(x,\mathfrak{t})}\widehat{S}=(\mathfrak{t}^{*})^{N(T)/T}\subset\mathfrak{t}^{*}.

Proof.

We note the following the inclusions:

T_{x}\mathfrak{S}/T_{x}S\subset T_{x}\Sigma/T_{x}S\subset T_{x}M/T_{x}S,

which have the Lie theoretical interpretation (see (
3.14
) and (
3.24
)):

(\mathfrak{z}(\mathfrak{g})^{*})^{G/G^{0}}\subset\mathfrak{z}(\mathfrak{g})^{*}\subset\mathfrak{g}^{*}.

So item (i) follows.

As for item (ii), we consider the holonomy action of
\pi_{1}(\widehat{S})
on the normal space to the presymplectic leaf. By Proposition
3.21
, this action coincides with the coadjoint action of the group of connected components of the isotropy of the groupoid
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}
, i.e.,
N(T)/T
. With the identifications described before the proposition, (ii) follows.
∎

To conclude the proof of (
4.20
) and the proof of the theorem, we only need to show that:

(\mathfrak{t}^{*})^{N(T)/T}=(\mathfrak{z}(\mathfrak{g})^{*})^{G/G^{0}}.

This follows from the short exact sequence (
4.9
), the decomposition (
3.8
) for
\mathfrak{t}^{*}
, and the fact that
W^{0}
acts without fixed points on
\mathfrak{t}^{{\mathrm{ss}}}:=[\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g}]\cap\mathfrak{t}
.
∎

4.6.
Weyl resolution of DMCTs

The definition of the Weyl resolution of a
\phi
-twisted DMCT
(M,L)
is exactly the same as for PMCTs, using maximal tori in the isotropy Lie algebras of
L
(see Definition
4.1
). We shall denote the resolution by

{\mathscr{R}}:(\widehat{M},\widehat{L})\to(M,L).

where
\widehat{L}={\mathscr{R}}^{*}L
is a twisted Dirac structure with twist
\widehat{\phi}={\mathscr{R}}^{*}\phi
.

Example 4.20
.

Let
G
be a compact connected Lie group, let
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
be an
\Ad
-invariant inner product on
\mathfrak{g}^{*}
, and let
L_{G}
be the corresponding
Cartan-Dirac structure on
G
with twisting
\phi
the Cartan 3-form
[
2
,
8
,
42
]
. Recall that its leaves are the conjugacy classes of
G
. An s-proper integration is provided by the conjugacy action groupoid
G\ltimes G
endowed the with the multiplicative 2-form

\Omega_{G}(g,h)=\frac{1}{2}\left(\langle\Ad_{h}{\mathrm{pr}}_{1}\theta^{L},{\mathrm{pr}}_{1}\theta^{L}\rangle+\langle{\mathrm{pr}}_{1}\theta^{L},{\mathrm{pr}}_{2}(\theta^{L}+\theta^{R}\rangle\right),

where
\theta^{L}
and
\theta^{R}
are the left and right-invariant Maurer-Cartan forms.

Similarly to the case of coadjoint orbits, recalling that the maximal torus of
G_{g}
are precisely the maximal torus of
G
that contain
g
, one finds that the resolution

\widehat{G}=\{(h,T)\,|\,h\in T,\,T\subset G\,\,\mathrm{maximal}\,\,\mathrm{torus}\},\quad{\mathscr{R}}=\mathrm{pr}_{1}:\widehat{G}\to G.

Fixing a maximal torus
T
, one has an identification

G\times_{N(T)}T=G/T\times_{W}T\cong\widehat{M},\quad[(g,h)]\mapsto(ghg^{-1},gTg^{-1}),

so under this identification the resolution map is given by:

{\mathscr{R}}([g,h])=ghg^{-1}.

The resolution enjoys many of the properties we saw for the case of PMCTs. For example, we have:

Theorem 4.21
.

For any twisted Dirac manifold of (strong)
\mathcal{C}
-type
(M,L)
:

(i)

(\widehat{M},\widehat{L})
is a regular Dirac manifold of (strong)
\mathcal{C}
-type;

(ii)

{\mathscr{R}}
is a forward Dirac map which restricts to a diffeomorphism from
{\mathscr{R}}^{-1}(M^{{\mathrm{reg}}})
to
M^{{\mathrm{reg}}}
;

(iii)

{\mathscr{R}}
descends to a homemorphism between the leaf spaces of
(\widehat{M},\widehat{L})
and
(M,L)
.

(iv)

For any twisted presymplectic integration
(\mathcal{G},\Omega)\rightrightarrows(M,L)
of (strong)
\mathcal{C}
-type, the action groupoid and the pullback of
\Omega

(4.22)

\widehat{\mathcal{G}}:=\mathcal{G}\ltimes\widehat{M}\rightrightarrows\widehat{M},\quad\widehat{\Omega}:={\mathrm{pr}}_{\mathcal{G}}^{*}\Omega,

is a twisted presymplectic integration of
(\widehat{M},\widehat{L})
of (strong)
\mathcal{C}
-type.

The proof of these facts is the same as in the Poisson case using the Hamiltonian local form for DMCTs (cf. Theorem
2.5
). More specifically, one shows that the Weyl resolution
\widehat{M}
again is canonically a closed embedded submanifold of
\mathrm{Gr}_{r}(T^{*}M)
, where
r
is the codimension of a regular leaf on
(M,L)
. The (regular) Dirac structure
\widehat{L}
is also obtained as the image of the injective bundle map

(\widehat{\mathscr{a}},\mathrm{d}{\mathscr{R}}^{*}\mathrm{pr}_{T^{*}M}):{\mathscr{R}}^{*}L\to T\widehat{M}\oplus T^{*}\widehat{M},

where
\widehat{\mathscr{a}}
is the action map.

The resolution map enjoys properties analogous to the ones of the resolution map in the Poisson case. Namely, one finds that:

(i)

{\mathscr{R}}:\widehat{M}\to M
is a proper smooth surjection with connected fibers;

(ii)

All values of
{\mathscr{R}}
are clean and the regular values are exactly the points in
M^{\mathrm{reg}}
;

(iii)

The range and kernel of
\mathrm{d}{\mathscr{R}}
satisfy:

\displaystyle{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathrm{d}_{x,\mathfrak{t}_{x}}{\mathscr{R}})^{0}=[\mathfrak{t}_{x},\mathfrak{g}_{x}],

\displaystyle\dim(\Ker\mathrm{d}_{x,\mathfrak{t}_{x}}{\mathscr{R}})=\dim\mathfrak{g}_{x}-\dim\mathfrak{t}_{x};

(iv)

Upon restriction to the twisted presymplectic leaves,

{\mathscr{R}}:\widehat{S}\to S

is a fiber bundle.

Moreover, the characterization of the Weyl resolution in Theorem
4.14
extends to DMCTs replacing the algebroid
T^{*}M
by
L
.

Finally, the resolution interacts with the canonical stratifications of
(M,L)
and
(\widehat{M},\widehat{L})
in an similar fashion as in the Poisson case.

Theorem 4.22
.

Let
(M,L)
be a twisted Dirac manifold of proper type. Then for each stratum
\Sigma
of
\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{inf}}(M,L)
, one has:

(i)

The intersection of
{\mathscr{R}}
with
\Sigma
is very clean;

(ii)

{\mathscr{R}}^{-1}(\Sigma)
is an embedded submanifold of
\widehat{M}
along which
{\mathscr{R}}
has constant rank equal to
\dim M+\operatorname{corank}(L)-\dim\mathfrak{g}_{x}
, for any
x\in\Sigma
;

(iii)

The stratification
{\mathscr{R}}^{-1}(\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{inf}}(M,L))
is induced by the partition of
\widehat{M}
given by the rank of
\mathrm{d}{\mathscr{R}}
, after passing to connected components;

(iv)

One has

\displaystyle\codim(\Sigma)-\codim({\mathscr{R}}^{-1}(\Sigma))

\displaystyle=\dim(\mathfrak{g}_{x})-\dim(\mathfrak{t}_{x}),

\displaystyle\codim({\mathscr{R}}^{-1}(\Sigma))

\displaystyle=\dim(\mathfrak{t}_{x})-\dim(\mathfrak{z}(\mathfrak{g}_{x})),

and in particular each subregular infinitesimal strata is of codimension 1.

Moreover,
{\mathscr{R}}
intersects every canonical stratum very cleanly and

\mathcal{S}(\widehat{M},\widehat{L})=\left\{{\mathscr{R}}^{-1}(\mathfrak{S}):\mathfrak{S}\in\mathcal{S}(M,L)\right\}

5.
The Geometry of the Leaf Space

In this section we fix a Poisson manifold of proper type
(M,\pi)
and we look at the structure present on its space of symplectic leaves
M/\mathcal{F}_{\pi}
. One of the main ingredients in this discussion is the resolution map
{\mathscr{R}}
. By Theorem
4.8
, it induces a homeomorphism

{\mathscr{R}}:\widehat{M}/\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{\pi}\to M/\mathcal{F}_{\pi}.

We will soon identify the two leaf spaces,
but we would like to point out that this identification is not entirely obvious and it becomes relevant when we take into account the various structures present on them. For example, when
M=\mathfrak{g}^{*}
, the dual of the Lie algebra of a compact Lie group
G
, this amounts to the identification of
\mathfrak{t}^{*}/W
with
\mathfrak{g}^{*}/G
.

Remark 5.1

(Leaf spaces of DMCTs)
.

All results in this section are valid for any twisted Dirac manifold of proper type. We leave it to the reader to check that the proofs hold in this more general setting.

5.1.
The smooth structure and a Chevalley-type theorem

The quotient map
M\to M/\mathcal{F}_{\pi}
can be used to endow
M/\mathcal{F}_{\pi}
not only with a topology, but also to make sense of “smooth functions”: we say that
f:M/\mathcal{F}_{\pi}\to\mathbb{R}
is smooth if its composition with the quotient map is smooth. We denote by
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(M/\mathcal{F}_{\pi})
the collection of all such smooth functions, and we define
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(\widehat{M}/\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{\pi})
similarly. The identification between the two leaf spaces is compatible with these smooth structures.

Theorem 5.2
.

The resolution map induces an isomorphism

{\mathscr{R}}^{*}:\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(M/\mathcal{F}_{\pi})\cong\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(\widehat{M}/\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{\pi}).

Proof.

Denote
B=M/\mathcal{F}_{\pi}
. The concept of smoothness defined above gives rise to a subsheaf
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}_{B}
of the sheaf
\mathcal{C}_{B}
of continuous functions on
B
. Similarly for
\widehat{B}=\widehat{M}/\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{\pi}
. The homeomorphism
{\mathscr{R}}:\widehat{B}\to B

gives rise to an isomorphism of sheaves

{\mathscr{R}}^{*}:{\mathscr{R}}^{*}\mathcal{C}_{B}\to\mathcal{C}_{\widehat{B}}.

The statement of the theorem follows from the stronger claim that this map restricts to an isomorphism

{\mathscr{R}}^{*}:{\mathscr{R}}^{*}\mathcal{C}^{\infty}_{B}\to\mathcal{C}^{\infty}_{\widehat{B}}.

This more stronger statement is sheaf theoretic and, therefore, it suffices to prove it locally. For that we can use the Hamiltonian local form to reduce it to the case where
M=\mathfrak{g}^{*}
, where
\mathfrak{g}
is a Lie algebra of compact type.

We are left to show that we have an isomorphism

(5.1)

{\mathscr{R}}^{*}:C^{\infty}(\mathfrak{g}^{*})^{G}\to C^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathfrak{g}^{*}})^{G},

where
G
is a compact Lie group with Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
, the resolution of
\mathfrak{g}^{*}
is (see Example
4.3
):

\widehat{\mathfrak{g}^{*}}=G/T\times_{W}\mathfrak{t}^{*},

and the resolution map is (cf. (
4.8
))

{\mathscr{R}}:G/T\times_{W}\mathfrak{t}^{*}\to\mathfrak{g}^{*},\quad(gT,\xi)\mapsto\Ad_{g}^{*}(\xi).

Here the
G
-action on
\widehat{\mathfrak{g}^{*}}=G/T\times_{W}\mathfrak{t}^{*}
is by translations on the first factor

g\cdot(g^{\prime}T,\xi)=(gg^{\prime}T,\xi).

By classical results of Chevalley (
[
27
, Section 3.5]
and Schwarz (
[
39
]
), the inclusion
i:\mathfrak{t}^{*}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{g}^{*}
gives an isomorphism

i^{*}:C^{\infty}(\mathfrak{g}^{*})^{G}\to C^{\infty}(\mathfrak{t}^{*})^{W}.

Now we just have to observe that the composition

{\mathscr{R}}^{*}\circ(i^{*})^{-1}:C^{\infty}(\mathfrak{t}^{*})^{W}\to C^{\infty}(G/T\times_{W}\mathfrak{t}^{*})^{G},

is clearly an isomorphism since it
maps a
W
-invariant function
f\in C^{\infty}(\mathfrak{t}^{*})
to the
G
-invariant function
\widehat{f}\in C^{\infty}(G/T\times_{W}\mathfrak{t}^{*})
given by

\widehat{f}([gT,\xi]):=f(\xi).

∎

Remark 5.3
.

The notion of smoothness on the leaf space introduced above makes sense whenever we deal with quotients of manifolds. In general, the resulting concept is a poor one. However, we are in a very special situation: both
M/\mathcal{F}_{\pi}
and
\widehat{M}/\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{\pi}
arise as orbit spaces of proper groupoids. That implies that that
C^{\infty}(M/\mathcal{F}_{\pi})
behaves very similarly to the algebra of smooth functions on a manifold, including the existence of partitions of unity.
Actually, for such orbit spaces even more is true: there are several existing frameworks that allow us to treat them as “smooth spaces”, such as that of ringed spaces, differentiable spaces, locally
C^{\infty}
-schemes, Sikorski spaces or subcartesian spaces (for an overview, and more references, see
[
14
]
). All these settings produce the same notion of smoothness as the one described above. However, as we shall see in the next section, the case of Poisson manifolds of proper type and their proper symplectic groupoids is even more special: the resulting leaf spaces are actually orbifolds.

Definition 5.4
.

Given a a Poisson manifold of proper type, we denote its leaf space by

(5.2)

B=B(M,\pi):=M/\mathcal{F}_{\pi}=\widehat{M}/\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{\pi}.

where the last equality is the identification induced by
{\mathscr{R}}
.

5.2.
Orbifolds and stratifications

Before continuing our discussion on the leaf space
B
we recall some basic notions and facts concerning orbifolds and their stratifications.

As in
[
12
, Chp. 2.6]
, by an
orbifold
we mean a pair
(B,\mathcal{B})
where
B
is a topological space and
\mathcal{B}
is an orbifold atlas for
B
, i.e.,
\mathcal{B}\rightrightarrows M
is a proper foliation groupoid and
p:M\to B
is a map inducing a homeomorphism
M/\mathcal{B}\to B
. By a
classical orbifold
we mean an orbifold presented by an effective orbifold atlas. Two atlases induce the same orbifold structure if they are Morita equivalent. By the discussion in Section
3.1.3
, an orbifold
B
carries an induced stratification, independent of the atlas, known as the
canonical stratification of the orbifold
B
.

A connected orbifold will be said to be
of smooth type
if its stratification has a single strata. This condition can be rephrased by requiring
B
to carry a smooth structure, necessarily unique, making
p:M\to B
into a smooth submersion. The same condition is equivalent also to the requirement that
\mathcal{B}
is
totally ineffective
, meaning that the normal actions of the isotropy groups
\mathcal{B}_{x}
are all trivial. Notice however that, even if
B
is of smooth type, it may still carry interesting non-trivial orbifold structures.

More generally, assume that a space
B
carries two different orbifold structures defined by atlases
\mathcal{B}_{i}\rightrightarrows N_{i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p_{i}}}{{\to}}B
. We will say that
\mathcal{B}_{1}
covers
\mathcal{B}_{2}
if there exist a surjective, submersive, groupoid morphism fibered over
B
, as in the following diagram:

\textstyle{\mathcal{B}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{B}_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{N_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p_{1}}
\textstyle{N_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p_{2}}
\textstyle{B}

We will also say that the second orbifold structure is an
isotropy reduction
of the first one. In this situation, the canonical stratifications induced by
\mathcal{B}_{1}
and
\mathcal{B}_{2}
coincide.

Given an orbifold
B
, with orbifold atlas
\mathcal{B}\rightrightarrows M
, by a
suborbifold
we mean any subspace
C\subset B

with the property that
p^{-1}(C)\subset M
is a submanifold of
M
. We say that
C
is
embedded
if
p^{-1}(C)\subset M
is an embedded submanifold. In this case we endow
C
with the orbifold structure presented by the restriction of
\mathcal{B}\rightrightarrows M
to
p^{-1}(C)
. Similarly, given a classical orbifold
B
with effective atlas
\mathcal{B}\rightrightarrows M
we say that
C\subset B
is a
classical embedded suborbifold
if
p^{-1}(C)\subset M
is an embedded submanifold and the restriction of
\mathcal{B}
to
p^{-1}(C)
is effective.

Remark 5.5
.

We warn the reader that the notion of suborbifold considered here is one suited for our purposes. There are various notions of suborbifold in the literature with subtle relationships – see, e.g.,
[
31
]
and references therein.

The previous discussion allows us to talk about
orbifold stratifications
: in Definition
3.3
the strata are allowed to be orbifolds, but they are required to be embedded suborbifolds.

Example 5.6
.

For any orbifold
B
the members of its canonical stratification are embedded suborbifolds of smooth type. So this stratification can be consider either as:

•

a stratification in the weaker sense of Definition
3.3
(so we regard its members as manifolds), or

•

an orbifold stratification (so we now regard its members as embedded suborbifolds).

From now on, given an orbifold we always consider its canonical stratification as an orbifold stratification.

Finally, here is one more concept on orbifolds that we need:
an
integral affine orbifold
is described by an orbifold atlas
\mathcal{B}\rightrightarrows M\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\to}}B

together with a transverse integral affine structure on the orbit foliation of
\mathcal{B}
. The latter can also be described by a presymplectic torus bundle
(\mathcal{T},\Omega_{\mathcal{T}})\to M
– see
[
12
, Prop. 3.2.8]
and Remark
3.31
.
An
integral affine suborbifold
is any suborbifold
C
with the property that
p^{-1}(C)\subset M
is
a transverse integral affine submanifold. This allows us to talk about
integral affine orbifold stratifications
of an integral affine orbifold: the strata are required to be integral affine suborbifolds.

5.3.
The integral affine orbifold structure

Using the identification (
5.2
), we now make actual use of the geometry of the resolution
\widehat{M}
, a
regular
Dirac manifold. Recall that a choice of proper integration
(\mathcal{G},\Omega)
of
(M,\pi)
gives rise to a
regular
, proper, presymplectic groupoid
(\widehat{\mathcal{G}},\widehat{\Omega})
over
\widehat{M}
(see (
4.22
)). This allows us to apply the Dirac version of Theorem 3.0.1 of
[
12
]
, obtaining the following result.

Theorem 5.7
.

Each proper symplectic integration
(\mathcal{G},\Omega)
of a Poisson manifold of proper type
(M,\pi)
induces an integral affine orbifold structure on the leaf space
B=B(M,\pi)
.

Let us explain briefly how the integral affine orbifold structure on the leaf space arises, using the concepts recalled in Section
5.2
.
The orbifold atlas for
B
is the proper foliation groupoid
\mathcal{B}(\widehat{\mathcal{G}})
defined by the short exact sequence

(5.3)

\textstyle{1\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{({\widehat{\mathcal{T}}},\widehat{\Omega}_{{\widehat{\mathcal{T}}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(\widehat{\mathcal{G}},\widehat{\Omega})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{B}(\widehat{\mathcal{G}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{1.}

Here
{\widehat{\mathcal{T}}}
is the bundle of groups consisting of the unit connected components of the isotropy groups of
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}
. Explicitly, it is the tautological torus bundle whose fiber at
(x,\mathfrak{t}_{x})\in\widehat{M}
is the torus
T_{x}\subset\mathcal{G}_{x}
integrating
\mathfrak{t}_{x}
. The restriction of
\widehat{\Omega}
to
{\widehat{\mathcal{T}}}
makes it into a presymplectic torus bundle which induces an integral affine structure on the orbifold.

From now on we always consider
B
with the integral affine orbifold structure on
B
induced by an integration
(\mathcal{G},\Omega)
.

5.4.
Stratifications of the leaf space of a Poisson manifold of proper type

Throughout this section
(M,\pi)
is a Poisson manifold of proper type, with a fixed choice of proper integration
(\mathcal{G},\Omega)\rightrightarrows(M,\pi)
. Our aim is to explain how the stratifications discussed in Section
3
interact with the integral affine orbifold structure that we just described.

First of all, the canonical and the infinitesimal stratifications of
(M,\pi)
descend to similar partitions on the leaf space
B
, denoted

\mathcal{S}(B),\quad\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{inf}}(B).

Via the identification (
5.2
) these can also be seen as induced by the canonical and the rank stratifications of the resolution
(\widehat{M},\widehat{L}_{\pi})
,
respectively – see Theorems
4.18
and
4.16
. Recalling the notions of “(integral affine) orbifold stratification” and “canonical stratification of an orbifold” (see Section
5.2
) one has:

Theorem 5.8
.

The members of
\mathcal{S}(B)
and
\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{inf}}(B)
carry natural orbifold structures so that
\mathcal{S}(B)
and
\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{inf}}(B)
become integral affine orbifold stratifications on the leaf space
B
.

Applying Corollary
4.17
we deduce:

Corollary 5.9
.

For any subregular strata
\Sigma\in\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{inf}}(M,\pi)
,
B_{\Sigma}:=\Sigma/\mathcal{F}_{\pi}\in\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{inf}}(B)
is a connected codimension one integral affine embedded suborbifold of
B
.

From now on, unless otherwise specified, we consider
\mathcal{S}(B)
and
\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{inf}}(B)
as integral affine orbifold stratifications, as in Theorem
5.8
. These have the following properties:

Proposition 5.10
.

Fix
\Sigma\in\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{inf}}(B)
. Then:

(i)

\mathcal{S}(B)
coincides with the canonical stratification of the orbifold
B
;

(ii)

The restriction
\mathcal{S}(B)|_{B_{\Sigma}}
is the canonical stratification of the orbifold
B_{\Sigma}
.

Each member of
\mathcal{S}(B)
and
\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{inf}}(B)
carries
another
natural orbifold structure, which we now describe. First, the members can be described as:

\displaystyle B_{\mathfrak{S}}:=B(\mathfrak{S},\pi|_{\mathfrak{S}})=\mathfrak{S}/\mathcal{F}_{\pi}={\widehat{\mathfrak{S}}}/\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{\pi},\quad

\displaystyle\textrm{with}\ \mathfrak{S}\in\mathcal{S}(M,\pi),\ {\widehat{\mathfrak{S}}}={\mathscr{R}}^{-1}(\mathfrak{S}),

\displaystyle B_{\Sigma}:=B(\mathfrak{S},\pi|_{\Sigma})=\Sigma/\mathcal{F}_{\pi}={\widehat{\Sigma}}/\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{\pi},\,\quad

\displaystyle\textrm{with}\ \Sigma\in\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{inf}}(M,\pi),\ {\widehat{\Sigma}}={\mathscr{R}}^{-1}(\Sigma).

Each member
\mathfrak{S}\in\mathcal{S}(M,\pi)
sits inside a member
\Sigma\in\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{inf}}(M,\pi)
, so that

B_{\mathfrak{S}}\subset B_{\Sigma}\subset B.

Theorem
3.18
and Theorem
3.29
ensure that
B_{\mathfrak{S}}
and
B_{\Sigma}
are themselves leaf spaces of regular PMCTs. Furthermore, as we shall also recall below, the proofs of those theorems show that the original groupoid
(\mathcal{G},\Omega)
induces proper symplectic integrations
\mathcal{G}_{\Sigma}
and
\mathcal{G}_{\mathfrak{S}}
of the strata
\Sigma
and
\mathfrak{S}
. Therefore both
B_{\Sigma}
and
B_{\mathfrak{S}}
inherit integral affine orbifold structures from
\mathcal{G}
. These are
different
from the integral affine orbifold structures appearing in Theorem
5.8
, which were inherited from
B
. To explain the relationship between the two integral orbifold structures on
B_{\Sigma}
and
B_{\mathfrak{S}}
we make use of the notion of “isotropy reduction” described in Section
5.2
.

Proposition 5.11
.

Let
\mathfrak{S}\in\mathcal{S}(M)
and
\Sigma\in\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{inf}}(M)
. Then:

(i)

The orbifold structures of
B_{\Sigma}
and
B_{\mathfrak{S}}
induced from
\mathcal{G}
are isotropy reductions of the orbifold structures inherited from
B
;

(ii)

If
B_{\mathfrak{S}}
and
B_{\Sigma}
are endowed with the orbifold structures induced from
\mathcal{G}
and
\mathfrak{S}\subset\Sigma
, then
B_{\mathfrak{S}}
is an integral affine suborbifold of
B_{\Sigma}
.

(iii)

If we endow the members of both
\mathcal{S}(B)
and
\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{inf}}(B)
with the orbifold structures induced from
\mathcal{G}
, then
\mathcal{S}(B)|_{B_{\Sigma}}
is the canonical orbifold stratification of
B_{\Sigma}
.

Remark 5.12
.

Notice that Theorem
5.8
implies that for each
\mathfrak{S}\in\mathcal{S}(B)
the orbifold structure on
B_{\mathfrak{S}}
induced from
B
is of smooth type (see Section
5.2
). The previous proposition then implies that the orbifold structure on
B_{\mathfrak{S}}
induced from
\mathcal{G}
is also of smooth type.

Proof of Theorem
5.8
and Propositions
5.10
and
5.11
.

We start by showing that both
\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{inf}}(B)
and
\mathcal{S}(B)
are integral affine orbifold stratifications of
B
.
The members of both partitions satisfy the frontier condition as in Remark
3.8
. So it suffices to prove that each

B_{\Sigma}={\widehat{\Sigma}}/\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{\pi},\quad B_{\mathfrak{S}}={\widehat{\mathfrak{S}}}/\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{\pi}

is an embedded integral affine suborbifold of
B
.

Checking that they are embedded suborbifolds is immediate: the preimage of
B_{\Sigma}
and
B_{\mathfrak{S}}
by

p:\widehat{M}\to B
are the embedded submanifolds
{\widehat{\Sigma}}
and
{\widehat{\mathfrak{S}}}
, respectively.

Checking the integral affine suborbifold condition is equivalent, by Remark
3.31
, to the presymplectic torus bundle
({\widehat{\mathcal{T}}},\widehat{\Omega})
being suited for smooth reduction along both
{\widehat{\Sigma}}
and
{\widehat{\mathfrak{S}}}
. The latter conditions are the same as
(\widehat{\mathcal{G}},\widehat{\Omega})
being suited for smooth reduction along these strata. For this observe that:

-

By Theorem
4.18
the strata
{\widehat{\mathfrak{S}}}
belongs to the canonical stratification of
(\widehat{M},\widehat{L}_{\pi})
. The Dirac version of Theorem
3.29
(see Section
3.4
) then shows that
(\widehat{\mathcal{G}},\widehat{\Omega})
is suited for smooth reduction along
{\widehat{\mathfrak{S}}}
.

-

We claim that
(\widehat{\mathcal{G}},\widehat{\Omega})
is suited for smooth reduction along
{\widehat{\Sigma}}
. For that, we fix
{\widehat{x}}=(x,\mathfrak{t}_{x})\in{\widehat{\Sigma}}
and we need to show that the connected Lie subgroup
\mathcal{K}_{{\widehat{x}}}\subset\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{{\widehat{x}}}
integrating
(T_{{\widehat{x}}}{\widehat{\Sigma}})^{0}
is closed (see Lemma
3.19
). Notice that

\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{{\widehat{x}}}=N(T)\subset\mathcal{G}_{x},

where
T\subset\mathcal{G}_{x}
is the maximal torus corresponding to
\mathfrak{t}_{x}
, and we claim that
\mathcal{K}_{{\widehat{x}}}
has Lie algebra

(5.4)

\mathfrak{k}_{{\widehat{x}}}=\mathfrak{g}_{x}^{\mathrm{ss}}\cap\mathfrak{t}_{x}\subset\mathfrak{g}_{x}.

It follows that
\mathcal{K}_{{\widehat{x}}}
is the intersection of a closed subgroup of
\mathcal{G}_{x}
with
N(T)
, hence it is closed.
To check (
5.4
) we recall that the Lie algebroid of
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}=\mathcal{G}\ltimes\widehat{M}
is identified with
\widehat{L}_{\pi}
via the map

(\mathscr{a},{\mathscr{R}}^{*}):{\mathscr{R}}^{*}(T^{*}M)\to\widehat{L}_{\pi}.

This restricts to an isomorphism

{\mathscr{R}}^{*}:\mathfrak{t}_{x}\to(T_{{\widehat{x}}}\widehat{S})^{0},

where
\mathfrak{t}_{x}\subset\mathfrak{g}_{x}=(T_{x}S)^{0}
. Now we note that the linear map

{\mathscr{R}}^{*}:(T_{x}S)^{0}\to(T_{{\widehat{x}}}\widehat{S})^{0},

is surjective with kernel
[\mathfrak{t}_{x},\mathfrak{g}_{x}]
(c.f. Proposition
4.6
). Since
{\mathscr{R}}
has very clean intersection with
\Sigma
, one finds

{\mathscr{R}}^{*}((T_{x}\Sigma)^{0})=(T_{{\widehat{x}}}{\widehat{\Sigma}})^{0}.

On the other hand, by (
3.13
), we have

(T_{x}\Sigma)^{0}=[\mathfrak{g}_{x},\mathfrak{g}_{x}]=\mathfrak{g}_{x}^{\mathrm{ss}}.

so (
5.4
) follows.

This completes the proof of Theorem
5.8
.

Next we prove item (i) of Proposition
5.10
. Using again that
{\mathscr{R}}
pulls back
\mathfrak{S}(M,\mathcal{F}_{\pi})

to
\mathfrak{S}(\widehat{M},\widehat{L}_{\pi})
, by the Dirac version of Proposition
3.22
(see Section
3.4
), the canonical stratification
\mathfrak{S}(\widehat{M},\widehat{L}_{\pi})
equals the holonomy stratification of the regular foliation
\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{\pi}
, and hence item (i) of Proposition
5.10
follows.

To prove item (ii) of Proposition
5.10
, we note that
{\widehat{\mathfrak{S}}}
and
{\widehat{\Sigma}}
are both saturated for the regular foliation
\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{\pi}
, so whenever
{\widehat{\mathfrak{S}}}\subset{\widehat{\Sigma}}
the holonomy of a leaf
\widehat{S}\subset{\widehat{\mathfrak{S}}}
for
\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{\pi}|_{{\widehat{\mathfrak{S}}}}
is the restriction of the holonomy of
\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{\pi}|_{{\widehat{\Sigma}}}
. Since the the canonical stratification
\mathcal{S}(\widehat{M})
equals the holonomy stratification of
\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{\pi}
, the result follows.

We now turn to the proof of Proposition
5.11
. To prove item (ii), we observe that by the proof of Theorem
3.29
, one has:

(a)

for any
\Sigma\in\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{inf}}(M)
, the reduction of
(\mathcal{G},\Omega)

is a proper symplectic integration
(\mathcal{G}(\Sigma),\Omega(\Sigma))
of
(\Sigma,\pi_{\Sigma})
:

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 5.5pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-5.5pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 29.5pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 29.5pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(\mathcal{T}({\Sigma}),\Omega(\Sigma)|_{\mathcal{T}(\Sigma)})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern
